relationship between the execution clocks attributed to the branch instruction and the
beginning of the target Id instruction prefetch. In reality, these two events do not occur
simultaneously as implied in Figure 7-11. The instruction unit performs all address cal-
culations for flow-control instructions during the decode stage for the branch instruction,
and the actual execution of the branch occurs in zero clocks. Thus, by the beginning of the
next clock (depicted as the first execute phase for the branch), the target address has
already been computed, and the address phase for the target instruction prefetch begins.
However, no other instructions can be dispatched for execution at this time. This 'dead'
clock is therefore attributed as one of the execution clocks for the branch. The second clock
for the branch-taken case is caused by the reply phase necessary for the prefetch of the
target instruction. Again, no other instruction can be dispatched for execution during this
clock if delayed branching is not used.

7.4.4.2 DELAYED BRANCH TIMING. Delayed branching is a feature that can be used for
more efficient execution of instruction sequences when branches are taken. Delayed
branching (invoked by specifying the .n option in a flow-control instruction) instructs the
processor to execute the instruction that sequentially follows the branch instruction before
the branch target instruction. The next sequential instruction has already been prefetched

by the time the branch executes; thus, it can execute while the instruction pipeline is
refilled with the instruction at the target address. Figure 7- 11 (b) shows the relative timing

afforded by the delayed branch featues is apparent by comparing the two diagrams in
Figure 7- 11. Figure 7-11 (a) shows that a branch taken with no delayed branching takes
two clocks to execute, and Figure 7-11 (b) shows that the delayed branch option allows
the add instruction (in the delay slot) to execute in one of those clocks. Therefore, the
effective execution time for branch instructions that use delayed branching is reduced to
one clock, providing the add is a useful instruction for the program. This time is the same
as that of the branch-not-taken time. The delay slot instruction following a jsr.n or bsr.n
can use the value in r1 with no scoreboard delay; the value is the result of the subroutine
call instruction.

static void debugmsg_va(struct cpu* cpu, int subsystem,
	const char *name, int verbosity, va_list argp, const char *fmt)
{
	bool subsystem_breakpoint = false;

	/*
	 *  #261: opt-in global break on any ERROR-level message (covers every
	 *  subsystem, incl. ones registered after a per-subsystem breakpoint
	 *  was set). Default off = single false test = no-op.
	 */
	bool do_bp = (debugmsg_n_breakpoints > 0 &&
	    debugmsg_breakpoint_level[subsystem] >= 0 &&
	    verbosity <= debugmsg_breakpoint_level[subsystem]);
	if (debugmsg_break_on_error && verbosity <= VERBOSITY_ERROR)
		do_bp = true;

	if (do_bp) {
		/*
		 *  This message triggers a breakpoint on subsystem messages:
		 *  make sure the message itself is shown (even if the current
		 *  verbosity setting would have suppressed it), and request
		 *  entry into the interactive debugger. The debugger is
		 *  entered after the current instruction batch, using the
		 *  same mechanism as normal breakpoints.
		 */
		subsystem_breakpoint = true;

		if (!single_step && !about_to_enter_single_step) {
			fatal("[ subsystem breakpoint: %s ]\n",
			    debugmsg_subsystem_name[subsystem][0] != '\0'
			    ? debugmsg_subsystem_name[subsystem] : "(startup)");
			about_to_enter_single_step = true;
		}
	}

	if (!subsystem_breakpoint && !single_step &&
	    !ENOUGH_VERBOSITY(subsystem, verbosity))
		return;

	char buf[DEBUG_BUFSIZE];

	buf[0] = buf[DEBUG_BUFSIZE - 1] = 0;
	vsnprintf(buf, DEBUG_BUFSIZE, fmt, argp);

	char* s = buf;
	bool ss = single_step || about_to_enter_single_step;
	bool debug_currently_at_start_of_line = true;
	bool show_decorations = emul_executing && !ss;

	if (console_are_slaves_allowed())
		show_decorations = false;

	while (true) {
		if (debug_currently_at_start_of_line) {
			if (show_decorations) {
				color_normal();
				printf("[ ");
			} else {
				for (size_t i = 0; i < debug_indent * DEBUG_INDENTATION; ++i)
					printf(" ");
			}

			bool print_subsystem_name =
rg: GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/*.h: IO error for operation on GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/*.h: The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect. (os error 123)

1013-	 *  reused.)

VERDICT: REQUEST CHANGES. The seven-class, two-switch delivery edit is sound, but the comments overstate architectural correctness, F14 is not closed, and the probe misses a return-path defect.

I verified `GXEMUL-SEC` at `83df95c`, the actual `47+ / 12−` diff, the detector, relevant manual passages and diagrams, and narrow record excerpts. Existing table-2 logs match the brief; the frozen probe’s MD5 begins `556aa357`. I ran no emulator, detector, gate, or battery. New counterexamples below are source predictions.

The executable changes belong together. Each `implemented = true` bypasses the first stop; each corresponding tail label bypasses the second stop; retaining `break` reaches `m88k_pc_to_pointers(cpu)`. Including BOUNDS and ERROR is justified.

| Class | Enumerated offset | Covering rows |
|---|---:|---|
| MISALIGNED | `8 × 4 = 32 = 0x20` | `misalign`; second fault in `error` |
| UNIMPLEMENTED | `8 × 5 = 40 = 0x28` | `unimpl` |
| PRIVILEGE | `8 × 6 = 48 = 0x30` | `priv`, `privtb` |
| BOUNDS | `8 × 7 = 56 = 0x38` | `bounds` |
| ILLEGAL DIVIDE | `8 × 8 = 64 = 0x40` | `div0`, `dslot`; first fault in `error` |
| OVERFLOW | `8 × 9 = 72 = 0x48` | `ovf` |
| ERROR | `8 × 10 = 80 = 0x50` | `error`, `errortb` |

The encodings check out: `f4436800` and `f4437004` select destination 2, source 3, and respectively source 0/divu or source 4/add; `14430000 >> 26 = 5`; the three `tb1` words select bit 0 and vectors 7, 8, 10. `80038023` stores r3 into CR1; `80404020` loads CR1 into r2; `f400fc92` matches `M88K_PROM_INSTR`. Anchors: `case 0x34: /* tb0 */`, `ic->arg[2] = iword & 0x1ff`, and `M88K_PROM_INSTR` in the fork’s decoder and header.

Within the checked source, **only `tb0/tb1/tcnd` naming 10 enter the first switch’s ERROR arm**. I also checked the computed memory callers: their arguments select vectors 2 or 3. Faults there can subsequently reroute to ERROR, after switch 1.

The residual list is correct:

- `11..113`: `113 − 11 + 1 = 103`.
- SFU1 vectors 114 and 115: 2.
- `116..127`: `127 − 116 + 1 = 12`.
- Total fallback population: **117**.

INFO is a reasonable level for delivered architectural exceptions. However, “unobservable” must mean **unobserved by this detector**. Verbosity filtering and configured subsystem/error breakpoints observe it. Anchor: `debugmsg_break_on_error && verbosity <= VERBOSITY_ERROR` in [debugmsg.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c).

The tail comment needs substantive corrections.

1. **Copying SXIP into SNIP alone is insufficient for sequential retry.** With the `div0` snapshot, SXIP is `0x10006`, SNIP `0x1000a`, SFIP `0x1000e`. Copying only SXIP into SNIP produces execution addresses `0x10004`, then `0x1000c`: it skips `0x10008`. A sequential retry needs the return pair arranged as SNIP `0x10006`, SFIP `0x1000a`. The manual explicitly describes arranging both pointers; the emulator’s `X(rte)` consumes both. Anchors: `6.4.3.1.2 Shadow Fetch Instruction Pointer` in [mc88100.txt](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/mc88100.txt), and `The NIP instruction should first be executed` in [cpu_m88k_instr.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c).

2. **The delay-slot snapshot exposes an actual return defect.** Enumerating the supplied row:

   | Quantity | Value |
   |---|---:|
   | Branch address | `0x10004` |
   | `c4000002` displacement | `2 × 4 = 8` |
   | Slot address | `0x10008` |
   | Target | `0x10004 + 8 = 0x1000c` |
   | Saved SNIP | `0x1000e` |
   | Saved SFIP | `0x1000e` |
   | Addresses after masking flags | both `0x1000c` |

   In `X(rte)`, `fip != nip + 4` is true: `0x1000c != 0x10010`. That arm executes the NIP instruction, then sets PC to FIP. Thus an ordinary instruction at the target executes **twice**. With a distinct branch target, the current snapshot instead introduces execution at the sequential fallthrough address before the target.

   This is inherited code, now reachable through delivery. The phrase “the common path above is already the architectural contract” cannot cover it. The requested measurement is **a delay-slot fault followed by handler return, with counters at fallthrough, target, and target+4**. Entry-register assertions alone cannot establish the execution sequence.

3. **ERROR needs an explicit qualification.** In `error`, the second fault occurs at `0x40`, but SXIP remains `0x10006`, describing the first fault. Therefore the blanket “SXIP = the faulting instruction” statement does not describe the current ERROR fault. Also, ERROR can arrive through a direct trap, with either frozen or unfrozen shadows.

4. **“SSBR stays 0” is too absolute.** The code assigns zero only inside `if (update_shadow_regs)`. With SFRZ already set, a previously nonzero SSBR survives. Say that scoreboard capture is not modelled and the shadow-update path writes zero.

These are anchored in `Perhaps something like this could work`, `if (update_shadow_regs)`, and the new `m88kexcdeliver` comments in [cpu_m88k.c](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_m88k.c).

For the remaining comment-to-observation mapping:

| Claim | Evidence and limit |
|---|---|
| Seven classes reach their entries without stopping | Ten fault rows; two-site necessity additionally supported by the mutation logs |
| `VBR + 8*vector` | Rows test **VBR = 0 only** |
| Sequential shadow values and MODE transition | Asserted; the three user-mode rows distinguish MODE setting |
| Destination unchanged | r2 asserted; the second fault in `error` targets **r6**, which is not asserted |
| Previously stopped every CPU | The source loop proves this; the detector exercises one CPU |
| Specifically a **user-mode divu** stops before the fix | Supported by the source; none of these rows executes user-mode divu |
| Handler execution, return, or successful retry | **No row** |
| INFO level, scoreboard, divide FCRs, residual vectors | Explicitly outside the assertions |

The default model PSR arithmetic is correct: `0x80000000 | 0x2 = 0x80000002`; adding exception bits `0x1 | 0x8` gives `0x8000000b`. Vector entries occupy eight bytes, consistent with the [Motorola manual’s vector-table description](https://www.bitsavers.org/components/motorola/88000/MC88100_RISC_Microprocessor_Users_Manual_2ed_1990.pdf).

**The smallest surviving edit is one character:** change the **tail guard** from `<` to `<=`:

```c
if (vector <= M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
```

All current fault rows remain green because they use vectors 4–10; both division controls remain green. But vector 128 now enters the tail default and stops: `8 × 128 = 1024 = 0x400`. The controls do not exercise an already-delivered exception.

If the mutation must touch an added line specifically, change PRIVILEGE’s `implemented = true` to `implemented = !is_trap`. Both `priv` and **`privtb` remain green**, because the privilege violation raised by the user-mode trap passes `is_trap = 0`. A supervisor trap directly selecting vector 6 would fail.

Also, the “11-row probe” count is stale: the current script has **10 fault programs + 2 controls = 12 program rows**, plus the parse self-test.

**F14 is the wrong disposition.** Comparing the entire PSR checks the tested carry value; it does not prove preservation of an initially set carry. Every row begins with carry clear. An unconditional carry clear in the new shared tail arm would survive all rows.

The missing overflow case should begin with PSR `0x90000002` and expect EPSR `0x90000002`, PSR `0x9000000b`, and unchanged destination. This directly distinguishes preservation from forced clearing. Anchor: `M88K_PSR_C` and the detector’s `PSR_RESET`/`PSR_EXC`.

Two other dispositions need corrections without reversing their central decisions:

- **F6:** 119 belongs to the earlier five-class arithmetic. The seven-class fallback count is **117**.
- **F15:** preservation of the tested shadows is correct, but the recognition diagram is **Figure 6-3**. Figure 6-4 is the `rte` flow. Section 6.10 independently supports frozen ERROR context.

I retain F3/D1’s inclusion decision. Reachability measurements establish that these paths occur; the manual supplies the reason to deliver them. I found no basis here to reverse the other dispositions, though I did not independently re-review every separately filed decoder issue.

Additional misses worth recording:

- **The delay-slot oracle has coincident addresses.** Replacing its SFIP assignment with the saved SNIP would survive because both equal `0x1000e`. Killing one M_DSLOT does not cover this alternative.
- **No relocated-VBR row exists.** Removing the VBR contribution would survive. A base of `0x20000` makes the divide entry `0x20040`.
- **No trap while SFRZ is already set is tested.** Moreover, the common path unconditionally clears DMT/DMD/DMA, despite the manual’s frozen-trap rule preserving exception-time and shadow registers. Anchor: `Default to no memory transactions`. This deserves a separate preservation finding; `error` does not establish it.
- **F11’s existing `m88kdivneg` record should include the `#195` comment.** Its claim that the MC88100 yields a wrapped result conflicts with the documented negative-operand divide exception. The host-arithmetic guard and the claimed architectural result are separate matters.

The records owe concrete updates in [CHANGELOG.md](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/CHANGELOG.md), [OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md), and the targeted `m88kexcdeliver` ledger entry:

- Preserve **upstream provenance**. The fallback history resolves to import `39748e3`; the batch-2 retrospective of #380 surfaced it. The #323/#380 fixes did not introduce it.
- Replace five-class scope with seven-class scope and record **both switch edits**. Correct “latent/no raise site” into “no literal-constant caller”; computed traps reach BOUNDS, ERROR, and the residual vectors.
- Correct the census. There are **11 first-switch bypasses**, including RESET; **10 non-stopping tail cases**, vectors 1–10; and **117 fallback vectors**. RESET adds another stopping vector. “8-of-128” is not the current count; it fits the earlier hypothetical `3 + 5` non-stopping-case count.
- Preserve the SFU1 split and disclosed register omissions. Record table 1’s ERROR-arm escape, the added `errortb` row, and table 2’s closure without rewriting the earlier result.
- Record the coverage corrections above, especially carry-set preservation and handler return.
- Preserve seat outcomes by name, attributed to the operator’s record: **GPT-6 Astra** failed twice with refusal outputs; **GLM-5.3 thinking** produced no answer before the answering mini re-fire; **Fable 5.1** hit its session limit and was re-fired for pass 2; **agy** was deliberately not fired. Failed seats are not agreement votes.
- The shipping detector path is currently absent, and I found no `M88KEXC` gate integration in this checkout. When adding it, require the self-test, both controls, and `10/10`: the standalone script currently prints failures without making its process exit status fail.
tokens used

```c
if (vector <= M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
```

All current fault rows remain green because they use vectors 4–10; both division controls remain green. But vector 128 now enters the tail default and stops: `8 × 128 = 1024 = 0x400`. The controls do not exercise an already-delivered exception.

If the mutation must touch an added line specifically, change PRIVILEGE’s `implemented = true` to `implemented = !is_trap`. Both `priv` and **`privtb` remain green**, because the privilege violation raised by the user-mode trap passes `is_trap = 0`. A supervisor trap directly selecting vector 6 would fail.

Also, the “11-row probe” count is stale: the current script has **10 fault programs + 2 controls = 12 program rows**, plus the parse self-test.

**F14 is the wrong disposition.** Comparing the entire PSR checks the tested carry value; it does not prove preservation of an initially set carry. Every row begins with carry clear. An unconditional carry clear in the new shared tail arm would survive all rows.

The missing overflow case should begin with PSR `0x90000002` and expect EPSR `0x90000002`, PSR `0x9000000b`, and unchanged destination. This directly distinguishes preservation from forced clearing. Anchor: `M88K_PSR_C` and the detector’s `PSR_RESET`/`PSR_EXC`.

Two other dispositions need corrections without reversing their central decisions:

- **F6:** 119 belongs to the earlier five-class arithmetic. The seven-class fallback count is **117**.
- **F15:** preservation of the tested shadows is correct, but the recognition diagram is **Figure 6-3**. Figure 6-4 is the `rte` flow. Section 6.10 independently supports frozen ERROR context.

I retain F3/D1’s inclusion decision. Reachability measurements establish that these paths occur; the manual supplies the reason to deliver them. I found no basis here to reverse the other dispositions, though I did not independently re-review every separately filed decoder issue.

Additional misses worth recording:

- **The delay-slot oracle has coincident addresses.** Replacing its SFIP assignment with the saved SNIP would survive because both equal `0x1000e`. Killing one M_DSLOT does not cover this alternative.
- **No relocated-VBR row exists.** Removing the VBR contribution would survive. A base of `0x20000` makes the divide entry `0x20040`.
- **No trap while SFRZ is already set is tested.** Moreover, the common path unconditionally clears DMT/DMD/DMA, despite the manual’s frozen-trap rule preserving exception-time and shadow registers. Anchor: `Default to no memory transactions`. This deserves a separate preservation finding; `error` does not establish it.
- **F11’s existing `m88kdivneg` record should include the `#195` comment.** Its claim that the MC88100 yields a wrapped result conflicts with the documented negative-operand divide exception. The host-arithmetic guard and the claimed architectural result are separate matters.

The records owe concrete updates in [CHANGELOG.md](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/CHANGELOG.md), [OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md), and the targeted `m88kexcdeliver` ledger entry:

- Preserve **upstream provenance**. The fallback history resolves to import `39748e3`; the batch-2 retrospective of #380 surfaced it. The #323/#380 fixes did not introduce it.
- Replace five-class scope with seven-class scope and record **both switch edits**. Correct “latent/no raise site” into “no literal-constant caller”; computed traps reach BOUNDS, ERROR, and the residual vectors.
- Correct the census. There are **11 first-switch bypasses**, including RESET; **10 non-stopping tail cases**, vectors 1–10; and **117 fallback vectors**. RESET adds another stopping vector. “8-of-128” is not the current count; it fits the earlier hypothetical `3 + 5` non-stopping-case count.
- Preserve the SFU1 split and disclosed register omissions. Record table 1’s ERROR-arm escape, the added `errortb` row, and table 2’s closure without rewriting the earlier result.
- Record the coverage corrections above, especially carry-set preservation and handler return.
- Preserve seat outcomes by name, attributed to the operator’s record: **GPT-6 Astra** failed twice with refusal outputs; **GLM-5.3 thinking** produced no answer before the answering mini re-fire; **Fable 5.1** hit its session limit and was re-fired for pass 2; **agy** was deliberately not fired. Failed seats are not agreement votes.
- The shipping detector path is currently absent, and I found no `M88KEXC` gate integration in this checkout. When adding it, require the self-test, both controls, and `10/10`: the standalone script currently prints failures without making its process exit status fail.
